  ### File tree

  ### Screens and routes (no router; `tab` state at main.jsx:22)
  Tabs are defined at main.jsx:17: today, practice, review, progress, account.
  - **Gates**
    - L81: loading splash.
    - L82: "beta is being prepared" when env is not configured.

    - Headline plus a "Continue" card.
    - "Remind me to review tomorrow" calls `LocalNotifications.requestPermissions()` then `schedule()` (L80). It schedules a single notification at now+24h, with id fixed at 1.
  - **Practice (L94–95)**
    - Subject picker over the 4 subjects.
    - Starts a fixed "quick", 5-question server session.
    - Runner with passage text, A–D options, prev/next, countdown timer, and submit.
  - **Review (L96)**
    - Shows score, `selectionMeta.recovery` (answer changes, recommendation title), and per-question explanations. Also lists past attempts.
    - Score formula is client-side: `correct*5 - wrong` per `total*5` (L96).
  - **Progress (L97)**
    - Placeholder text and a count of server-scored sessions.
  - **Account (L98)**
    - Email, "Free beta", and sign-out.
  
  None of the following exist: Explore, mock/NTA tests, study/learn, PrepOS AI, Admission Compass, leaderboard, saved, rival, profile, payments, Mistake Repair flow.
  
  ### Data access (website API, not direct Supabase DB)
  - Base URL is `VITE_API_BASE_URL` (main.jsx:14, 16).
  - Calls, each with `Authorization: Bearer <supabase access_token>`:
  
  | Call | Where | Website handler |
  |---|---|---|
  | `POST /api/mobile/login` | L84 | `src/app/api/mobile/login/route.js` re-exports `auth/email-login` (generates the OTP via Supabase admin and emails it via Resend) |
  | `GET /api/mobile/attempts` | L30 | re-exports `/api/attempts` |
  | `POST /api/sessions` | L72 | starts a session |
  | `PUT /api/sessions` | L65 | records events |
  | `PATCH /api/sessions` | L79 | submits |
  
  - `/api/mobile/me` and `/api/mobile/attempts/[id]` also exist server-side but are unused by the client.
  - The server wiring is in `src/lib/server/mobileCors.js` (14 lines).
    - Allowed origins are `capacitor://localhost` and `https://localhost`, plus `http://localhost:5173` in dev.
    - It sets `Cache-Control: no-store`.
    - `src/app/api/sessions/route.js` L11–32 handles POST/PATCH/PUT. It validates the bearer via `auth(request)` (`src/lib/auth.ts` L140–148 → `supabaseAdmin().auth.getUser(token)`), rate-limits 60 per user, and caps the payload at 600 KB.
  - Direct Supabase usage in the app is **auth only**: `verifyOtp` (L84), `getSession`, `onAuthStateChange`, `signOut`.
  
  ### Auth
  - Supabase client (L15) with `auth:{storage, persistSession:true, detectSessionInUrl:false, autoRefreshToken:true}`. The custom `storage` is the SecureStorage adapter, so the session lives in Keychain/Keystore on native.
  - OTP only: `/api/mobile/login` sends the email code, then `supabase.auth.verifyOtp({email,token,type:'email'})`.
  - **No Google sign-in.** The website supports it (`signInWithGoogle` in `src/lib/supabase-browser.js` L28, plus NextAuth Google) but the mobile app does not. No OAuth deep-link handling exists.
  - On app background/foreground it stops and starts Supabase auto-refresh (L38–40, via `@capacitor/app` `appStateChange`).
  - On sign-out or user switch, attempts, result and draft state are wiped (L37, L98). Cache keys are namespaced `reviews:<uid>` and `draft:<uid>`.
  
  ### Offline handling
  - `navigator.onLine` badge (L44–47, L91). The Start and Submit buttons are disabled when offline.
  - The draft session (questions plus event log) is persisted to SecureStorage on every answer or navigation (L61) and restored on launch (L51–58).
  - A 1.5 s interval (L69) retries `PUT /api/sessions` for the event log. The timer is wall-clock based.
  - Review history is cached in SecureStorage and used as a fallback when the fetch fails (L30–31).
  - This is a "sync events before expiry" design, not true offline practice. There is no service worker and no cached question bank.
  - Caveats:
    - SecureStorage (Keystore-backed, small-value oriented) is used for large JSON caches.
    - Event timestamps rely on the device clock (`Date.now() - startedAt`, where `startedAt` is server-provided).
  
  ### Shared code imports
  - `../../shared/recoveryContract` (`LAUNCH_SUBJECTS`, `createRecoveryApi`) and `../../shared/tokens.css`.
  - The website also imports `shared/recoveryContract` (`src/lib/server/recoverySessions.js:8`, `practiceSessions.js:8`, `src/app/(app)/recovery/page.jsx:8`, `src/app/api/recovery/replay/route.js:5`). That is a genuine shared contract.
  - **No imports from `../data`.**
  
  ### Known defects and gaps
  - **`createRecoveryApi` and `getToken` crash when unconfigured.** `getToken` dereferences `supabase.auth` (L16), but `supabase` is null when env is missing. This is only safe because the render guard returns first.
  - **Hardcoded sequential logic.** `start()` always sends `mode:'quick', count:5` (L72).
  - **Missing flows.** There is no "stay on question after expiry" or timer auto-submit flow. There are no error boundaries and no i18n.
  - **Accessibility and design.**
    - Plain CSS: 44–48 px targets and `aria-pressed` are present.
    - Not responsive beyond one 380 px breakpoint.
    - No dark theme, unlike the website.
  - **Tests.** No tests, no lint and no CI for mobile.

  ## 8. Honest assessment
  
  ### Distance from production: large (roughly 10–15%)
  
  **What works on paper**
  - Compiles and bundles cleanly. The architecture is sound: bundled SPA, bearer-token API reuse, CORS allowlist, secure storage, user-scoped caches, server-authoritative scoring.
  - The website backend already has the mobile endpoints and bearer auth (`auth(request)`), so server work for the happy path is mostly done.
  
  **Blocking or major gaps**
  1. **Never built or run.** No APK, no device, and no JDK/adb in the shell. The Gradle build is untested, so there may be surprises such as AGP 8.13 / Gradle 8.14.3 / Java 21 toolchain mismatches or SDK 36 availability.
  2. **`dist` has no env baked in**, so the shipped app shows only the "being prepared" screen. It needs `VITE_API_BASE_URL`, `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` and `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, and the API host (`mockmob.in` vs `www.mockmob.in`) must be confirmed.
  3. **Feature scope.** It covers only a 5-question diagnostic loop (4 subjects, fixed `mode:quick`). Missing: mocks/NTA, Explore, study/learn, Mistake Repair UI, PrepOS, Compass, profile, saved, and Razorpay or any billing. The Google Play billing policy for digital goods is not designed (SPEC says "free beta, no purchase links").
  4. **Auth.** No Google sign-in (the website's primary login path), no deep-link or OAuth return handling, and no account deletion (a Play Data safety requirement).
  5. **Release plumbing.** No signing config, no keystore, no flavors or debug id suffix, `minifyEnabled false`, `versionCode 1`, and still `in.mockmob.beta` (README itself says to set a unique production id).
  6. **Brand and store assets.** Splash and legacy icons are default Capacitor. There is no Play listing art, privacy policy URL wiring, or Data-safety mapping.
  7. **Play-policy risk.** `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` is pulled in by local-notifications for a once-a-day reminder. Drop it or switch to inexact scheduling, or file a declaration.
  8. **Quality gates.** No tests, lint or CI. The README itself lists the device matrix as "launch gates".
  9. **Architecture limits.**
     - One 100-line component with no routing, no component structure, and no focus or back-button handling (no Android hardware-back listener for `@capacitor/app`).
     - Large JSON caches in Keystore-backed SecureStorage.
     - Client-side score formula duplicated from the server.
     - No analytics or crash reporting.
     - No App Links.
  10. **Hygiene.**
      - Template remnants: `com.getcapacitor.myapp` tests, the Android-robot foreground, teal background.
      - `@capacitor/keyboard` is installed but unused.
      - `@capacitor/ios` is declared with no iOS project.
  
  ### Reuse
  - **Keep or reuse**
    - The overall approach: bundled Capacitor shell with the hosted API.
    - `shared/recoveryContract.js` and the API contract.
    - The server-side mobile endpoints, `mobileCors.js` and bearer `auth()`.
    - The cache-isolation and draft-persistence ideas.
    - `storage.js` as a pattern (but reconsider the storage backend).
    - Plugin choices (`app`, `local-notifications`, `secure-storage`) and the Android project skeleton (Capacitor 8, AGP 8.13).
    - The adaptive icon concept and bundled fonts.
    - The `.gitignore` rules for `ios/` and keystores.
  - **Rewrite or replace**
    - All UI in `main.jsx` and `style.css` (split into routed screens, real design system matching the website's `DESIGN.md`/dark theme).
    - Session runner and sync logic (extract hooks and a tested sync queue).
    - Splash, icons and branding.
    - The Android release config, applicationId and signing.
    - Anything reliant on `selectionMeta` shape assumptions without type contracts.
  
  ### Alternative worth weighing
  Because the website already ships a PWA manifest and standalone display, the fastest route to a Play Store listing may be a **TWA** (Bubblewrap), which needs `assetlinks.json` plus a verified domain. The docs explicitly chose the bundled-Capacitor route (to avoid a remote-URL wrapper) and never evaluated a TWA. Bundled Capacitor gives better offline and native control; TWA gives full website parity immediately. The existing scaffold supports the first choice only for its tiny feature set.
  
  ### Suggested minimum path if continuing with Capacitor
  1. Install the JDK and Android SDK, then do a real `cap sync` and debug build with real env.
  2. Verify the email OTP flow on a device (§7 "Required device checks").
  3. Add Google sign-in with deep links, a hardware-back handler, and branded splash and icon.
  4. Drop the exact-alarm permission and set the production applicationId and signing.
  5. Expand screens to parity with the web Today/Practice/Review/Progress.

  - Dev-only (call `notFound()` when `NODE_ENV==='production'`): `/preview/arena` (`ArenaPreviewClient.jsx`, 294, mock user), `/preview/brand`, `/preview/study`, `/preview/wall`, `/preview/question-factory`, `/preview/recovery` (redirects to `/preview/arena?view=result`).
  
  ### 1.7 Payment handlers outside `/api`
  `src/app/create-subscription/route.js` (154), `create-monthly-subscription/route.js` (103), `create-order/route.js` (re-exports create-subscription), `verify-payment/route.js` (294), `webhook/route.js` (538).
  
  ### 1.8 API routes by domain
  "Bearer" = calls `auth(request)`, so it accepts a Bearer token or cookie. "Cookie" = calls `auth()` with no argument, so cookie only.
  
  - **Auth/session:**
    - `auth/me` GET (Bearer)
    - `auth/email-login` POST (public, rate-limited; sends a Resend email with magic link and 8-digit OTP via `supabaseAdmin().auth.admin.generateLink`)
    - `auth/[...nextauth]` (see section 4)
    - `mobile/login`, `mobile/me`, `mobile/attempts`, `mobile/attempts/[id]` (thin wrappers that add CORS and `no-store`)
    - `users/[id]` GET/PATCH (Cookie)
    - `coming-soon/unlock`
  - **Practice sessions:**
    - `sessions` POST/PUT/PATCH/OPTIONS (Bearer, with CORS): start, record events, submit
    - `attempts` GET (Bearer; POST is a deprecated alias of the sessions PATCH), `attempts/[id]` GET (Bearer)
    - `practice/quote` GET (Bearer)
    - `subjects`, `chapters`, `stats` (public)
  - **Learning/recovery:**
    - `learning/plan` (Bearer, via `learningHttp`)
    - `learning/summary` (Cookie)
    - `recovery` GET/POST (Bearer), `recovery/replay` (Bearer), `recovery/repair` (Bearer)
    - `recovery/episodes`, `recovery/episodes/[id]/responses` (Bearer, via `learningHttp`)
    - `study/catalog`, `study/preferences` (GET/PUT), `study/runs`, `study/runs/[id]`, `study/runs/[id]/events`, `study/units/[id]` (all Bearer via `learningHttp`)
  - **PrepOS/AI:**
    - `ai/mentor/chat` (Bearer)
    - `ai/mentor/history`, `ai/mentor/local`, `ai/mentor/session/new`, `ai/context`, `ai/usage`, `ai/actions/execute` (Cookie)
    - `ai/credits` GET, `ai/credits/order`, `ai/credits/verify` (Cookie)
    - `ai/rival/start`, `ai/rival/submit` (Cookie)
    - `prepos/insights` (Bearer)
    - `compass/pro` (Bearer)
  - **Content/community:**
    - `questions` GET/POST, `questions/feed`, `questions/explore`, `questions/mine`, `questions/upload`, `questions/moderate`, `questions/[id]/interact`, `questions/[id]/vote`, `bookmarks` (all Cookie)
    - `questions/pending` (**no auth at all**, see section 8)
    - `leaderboard` (public, no auth)
    - `analytics` (Bearer; this is the Radar data endpoint, not event tracking)
  - **Billing:** `billing/cancel` (Cookie), `billing/webhook` (shared-secret header `x-mockmob-signature`, compared with `!==`), `credits/grant` and `credits/spend` (both return 410, disabled)
  - **Admin/internal:** `admin/{creators,creators/[id],orders,overview,payouts,question-factory}` (cookie via `requireAdmin`), `creator/me`, `internal/moderation/{process,recover}` (`INTERNAL_API_SECRET`), `send-email` (admin only, env-gated)

  ## 3. Design system
  
  **Where tokens live (three scoped systems):**
  1. `src/app/globals.css` (4,125 lines)
     - `@import "tailwindcss"` and `@theme inline`: `--color-volt #d2f000`, `--color-ink #0a0a0a`, `--color-ink-2 #141414`, `--font-display/body/mono`, marquee/pulse animations.
     - `:root` motion tokens: `--ease-out cubic-bezier(.23,1,.32,1)`, `--ease-in-out`, `--ease-drawer`, `--dur-press 140ms`, `--dur-ui 200ms`, `--dur-enter 420ms`.
     - Legacy dark globals: `--volt`, `--ink`, `--text-primary #f6f7ee`, `--text-muted`, `--focus-ring`.
     - Legacy classes: `.glass`, `.btn-volt`, `.eyebrow`, `.display-*`, `.q-chip`, `.skeleton`.
     - From about L1470, the **`.mm` marketing scope** with its own tokens: `--paper #f4f5f0`, `--paper-raised`, `--paper-sunken #eaece3`, `--ink #10140e`, `--ink-2`, `--ink-3`, `--line`, `--line-strong`, `--volt`, `--volt-edge #9db400`, `--volt-soft`, `--volt-ink`, `--slate #0f130e`, `--slate-2`, `--slate-3`, `--chalk`, `--flag`, radii `--r-1..4` (10/14/20/28), `--sh-1..3`, `--gutter` 20/32/40, `--rhythm` 56/88/112. Components include `.mm-btn` (a "keycap" button with a hard bottom edge via `--btn-edge`), `.mm-wrap`, `.mm-nav`, `.mm-dock`.
  2. `src/app/marketing-theme.css` (98): `.mm` light/dark overrides and theme-role tokens (`--accent-text`, `--accent-line`, `--tint`, `--warn-text`, `--bad-text`, `--good-text`). The theme toggle (sun/moon switch with a View Transition reveal) is styled here.
  3. `src/app/(app)/arena.css` (1,424): **`.app-shell` scope**, tokens `--a-bg #0b100e`, `--a-raised #131a16`, `--a-line`, `--a-ink*`, `--a-accent-text`, `--a-radius 18px`. The light theme remaps Tailwind's `--color-zinc-*`, `--color-white` and red/amber/green so existing `text-zinc-*` utilities keep working.
  - `shared/tokens.css` is only 5 variables (`--mm-paper`, `--mm-ink`, `--mm-ink-2`, `--mm-volt`, `--mm-slate`, `--mm-line`, `--mm-radius:12px`), used only by the mobile app. **The web and mobile token vocabularies differ.**
  - `DESIGN.md` (622 lines) contains a full token spec with front matter (colors, typography, rounded, spacing, components) and written rules (Two Volts, No-Halo, 44px floor, Phone-First, Thumb-Zone). `PRODUCT.md` states the device floor is a budget Android on patchy 4G.
  
  **Fonts:** Gabarito (display, 500-900), Hanken Grotesk (body), Geist Mono (numbers and labels), all via `next/font/google`. The mobile app bundles Gabarito and Hanken woff2 files.
  
  **Colours:** paper `#f4f5f0`, ink `#10140e`/`#172016`, volt `#d2f000` (fill with dark ink, never body text on paper; lime text `#526700`/`#4f6400` on light), night `#0b100e` / `#131a16`, slate `#0f130e`, flag `#b93225`, amber `#e0b93f`, coral `#e0685a`.
  
  **Dark/light:** `data-theme` on `<html>`, persisted in `localStorage['mm:theme:v1']`, default **light**, set by an inline head script. There is no `prefers-color-scheme` usage. `ThemeToggle.jsx` (87) is shared. The NTA console skin stays independent of the theme.
  
  **Motion:** the `motion` package (`motion/react`) is used in only 6 files: `ai/AssistantDrawer`, `ai/AssistantLauncher`, `ui/dot-pattern`, `ui/number-ticker`, `ui/scroll-based-velocity`, `ui/ShiningText`. Everything else is hand-written CSS plus IntersectionObserver and rAF (`landing/Reveal.jsx`, `FeatureTour`, `PipGuide`, `ResultPageClient.CountUp`). `docs/brain/MOTION.md` defines 3 tiers: storytelling on marketing, selection in the app, near-zero during answering. Reduced motion is honoured.
  
  **Icons (three coexisting approaches):**
  - `ui/Icons.jsx`: 27 hand-drawn inline SVGs, used by marketing and legacy pages.
  - `ui/Glyph.jsx`: lucide wrappers (AppIcon, StatusIcon, SubjectIcon, ModeIcon, CreditAmount) with a rule "one family, 1.75 stroke".
  - Direct `lucide-react` imports in 36 files.
  - Package versions: lucide-react 1.8.
  
  **Primitives in `src/components/ui/`:** Button, Card, Badge, Input, Avatar, Skeleton, StatCard, ProgressBar, PricingCard, SignupCard, Glyph, Icons, LiquidGlassButton, PrepOSOrb, ScrollReveal, ShiningText, number-ticker, morphing-text, scroll-based-velocity, dot-pattern, interactive-grid-pattern. Most are **not imported anywhere** (list in section 8). `src/lib/utils.js` carries clsx/tailwind-merge/cva, used only by `LiquidGlassButton`.
  
  **Chart library:** `chart.js` + `react-chartjs-2`, used in exactly one place (`AnalyticsPageClient.jsx`; it reads CSS variables at runtime because canvas cannot).
  
  ## 4. Auth and data access
  
  - **Live auth is Supabase.**
    - Browser: `src/lib/supabase-browser.js` (`createBrowserClient`, PKCE, `detectSessionInUrl:false`; the `/auth/callback` page owns the exchange).
    - Server: `src/lib/auth.ts` `auth(request?)`. With a Bearer header it calls `supabaseAdmin().auth.getUser(token)`; otherwise it reads the Supabase SSR cookies. It then upserts a `users` row by email (`ensureDbUserFromIdentity`) and returns `{user:{id,name,email,image,subjects,role,creditBalance,supabaseId}}`.
    - `AuthProvider.jsx` (298): `getSession` with retry, then `/api/auth/me` with 7 retries, exposing `user, status, needsOnboarding, signInWithGoogle, signInWithEmail, verifyEmailOtp, signOut`.
    - Sign-in methods: Google OAuth and email OTP (server generates link plus 8-digit code, sends it with Resend; client verifies with `verifyOtp` type `magiclink`, then `email`). Template: `supabase/templates/magic_link.html`.
  - **next-auth v5 beta is vestigial.** `src/lib/auth.ts` still configures Google and a "Demo Login" Credentials provider (name only, no password) and exports `handlers`, mounted at `/api/auth/[...nextauth]`. Nothing reads a next-auth session: `auth()` is Supabase-only. It still looks like a live attack surface (the Credentials `authorize` creates a DB user from a username, Google uses a `'mock'` fallback client id, and `NEXTAUTH_SECRET` falls back to a literal string). Worth confirming and removing.
  - **`middleware.js` (project root; `middleware` is the filename Next 16 deprecates in favour of `proxy`).**
    - Verified as registered in `.next/server/middleware-manifest.json`.
    - Matcher: `/dashboard/*`, `/test/*`, `/analytics/*`, `/pricing/*` only.
    - For the first three it calls Supabase `getUser()` and redirects unauthenticated users to `/signup`.
    - For `/pricing` it only captures `?ref=`/`?code=` into a 30-day `mm_ref` cookie.
    - It also logs structured JSON (masked IP, UA class).
    - **All other app routes (`/today`, `/review`, `/learn`, `/profile`, etc.) are protected only client-side** by `AppLayoutClient` (redirects to `/signup`). `/admin` and `/creator` guard server-side in the page.
  - **Client data access is API-only.** Browser Supabase is used only for auth calls. There is no direct client-to-Supabase table access. Routes use `supabaseAdmin()` (service role, bypasses RLS) via `src/lib/supabase.js` and `data/db.js` (a `Database` object of about 47 methods). Fetch wrappers: `src/lib/fetcher.js` (`api`, `apiGet`, `apiPost`, `apiPatch`); some components use raw `fetch`. Some pages still pass `?userId=` (ignored server-side).
  - **Bearer support for a native app:**
    - Works on every route listed "Bearer" in 1.8. That covers sessions, attempts, practice quote, recovery, learning plan, study, PrepOS chat and insights, compass pro, analytics and auth/me.
    - **Cookie-only (a native app cannot use these without changes):** bookmarks, all `/api/questions/*` (feed, explore, interact, vote, upload, mine), `ai/credits*`, `ai/mentor/{history,local,session/new}`, `ai/rival/*`, `ai/usage`, `ai/context`, `ai/actions/execute`, `learning/summary`, `users/[id]`, `billing/cancel`, and **all payment routes** (`create-subscription`, `create-monthly-subscription`, `verify-payment`).
    - Learning, study and recovery routes also run a 60 req/min persistent rate limit keyed on user id.
  - **CORS:** `src/lib/server/mobileCors.js` allows only origins `capacitor://localhost` and `https://localhost` (plus `http://localhost:5173` in dev), methods GET/POST/PUT/PATCH/OPTIONS, headers Authorization and Content-Type. It is applied only to `/api/sessions` and `/api/mobile/{login,me,attempts,attempts/[id]}`. Every other route returns no CORS headers, so a Capacitor WebView calling them would be blocked by the browser; a true-native HTTP client (RN, Flutter, Kotlin) would not be affected. `next.config.mjs` has no CORS configuration.
  - **`next.config.mjs`:**
    - `distDir` switches by env: `.next-staging`, `.next-dev` or `.next`.
    - `poweredByHeader:false`, `devIndicators:false`.
    - `outputFileTracingIncludes` for about 40 evidence/runtime files.
    - Redirect `/home` to `/` (301). A comment warns against adding a www/apex redirect (it caused a prod loop on 4 Oct 2026).
    - Headers, production only: immutable cache for static asset extensions; `/sw.js` no-cache.
    - **No CSP, X-Frame-Options, HSTS or similar security headers.**
  - **Rate limiting:** in-memory by default; Supabase `take_rate_limit` RPC only when `PERSISTENT_LIMITS_ENABLED==='true'`. On serverless the in-memory path is weak.
  - **Env var names used by code** (names only): `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `RAZORPAY_*`, `RESEND_API_KEY`, `AUTH_EMAIL_FROM`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL`, `PRACTICE_SESSION_SECRET`, `PERSISTENT_LIMITS_ENABLED`, `STUDY_*_ENABLED`, `RECOVERY_PATHWAYS_ENABLED`, `LEARNING_SESSIONS_ENABLED`, `INTERNAL_API_SECRET`, `BILLING_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `COMING_SOON_*`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_*`, `NEXTAUTH_*`, plus AI provider keys (`ANTHROPIC`, `OPENAI`, `DEEPSEEK`, `AI_*_MODEL`). `.env.local` exists locally with Supabase, Razorpay, Resend, Google and AI keys; values not read or reported.
  
  ## 5. PWA / app-related
  
  - **Manifest:** `src/app/manifest.js` (id `/`, `start_url:'/today'`, standalone, bg/theme `#0b100e`, icons `public/pwa/icon-192.png` and `icon-512.png` purpose "any" only). **No maskable icon, no apple-touch-icon, no screenshots, no shortcuts.** The `theme_color` in the manifest (dark) disagrees with the layout viewport themeColor (`#F4F5F0` light).
  - **Service worker:** `public/sw.js` (11 lines). Cache name `mockmob-public-fallback-v1`, caches only `/offline.html` and the two PWA icons, same-origin GET only, navigation falls back to `offline.html`. It explicitly never caches account HTML, RSC or APIs. **It is registered only from `src/components/InstallPractice.jsx`**, which is rendered only inside the full `LearningNextAction` on `/today`, only in production. So the SW installs only after a visitor opens Today.
  - **`public/offline.html`:** dark, static, links to `/today`, explains that the timer keeps running and drafts stay on-device.
  - **Install prompts:** only `InstallPractice` (a card on `/today`): `beforeinstallprompt` on Android/desktop, and an "Add to Home Screen" help line on iOS. There are no "download the app" banners and no App Store or Play links anywhere on the website. Footer social is Instagram only.
  - **Deep links:** none. No `public/.well-known/` folder (no `assetlinks.json`, no `apple-app-site-association`). `mobile/android/.../AndroidManifest.xml` has only MAIN/LAUNCHER and **no VIEW intent filter**. The mobile app registers no `appUrlOpen` handler; login uses the typed email code, and the email's magic link opens `/auth/callback` in a browser.
  - **Push:** none. The web does not use the Notification or Push API. The mobile app has only `@capacitor/local-notifications` (one scheduled local reminder, requested explicitly). There is no `@capacitor/push-notifications` and no `google-services.json`.
  - **Mobile build state:**
    - `mobile/capacitor.config.json` is minimal (appId, appName `MockMob Beta`, `webDir:dist`).
    - Android: minSdk 24, target/compile SDK 36, versionCode 1, `allowBackup=false`.
    - The README says no JDK/adb was available, so no APK or device test has been done.
  
  ## 6. Payments (Razorpay)
  
  - **Plans:** `src/lib/payments/plans.js`.
    - `pro_monthly_99`: Rs 99/month subscription, `totalCount` 24. It becomes sellable only when env `RAZORPAY_PLAN_ID_PRO_MONTHLY_99` is set (`isLiveMonthlyPlan`).
    - `pro_cuet_2027`: Rs 99 one-time, access through 2027-07-31, sold only while monthly billing is not configured.
    - `pro_cuet_2027_v2` (Rs 299) is gated by `newOfferReleased()`, currently false.
    - `pro_cuet_2026` and `pro_monthly` (Rs 69) are legacy.
    - Local `.env.local` has `RAZORPAY_PLAN_ID_PRO_MONTHLY` (legacy) but not `..._99`, so locally the one-time Rs 99 offer would show. Production config cannot be verified from the repo.
  - **What a visitor sees:** `src/lib/payments/offer.js` `publicOffer()` returns `purchasable: 'monthly' | 'one_time'`, used by the landing page, pricing page and practice quote.
  - **Client flow:** `src/components/billing/RazorpayPaymentButton.jsx` (351) loads `https://checkout.razorpay.com/v1/checkout.js`, calls `/api/auth/me` (401 sends the user to `/login`), then `POST /create-subscription` (orders) or `/create-monthly-subscription`. After checkout, the handler posts `razorpay_*` ids to `/verify-payment`. A referral code is prefilled from the URL, localStorage or the `mm_ref` cookie (`src/lib/referrals/offers.js`).
  - **Server:**
    - Both create routes call `auth()` (cookie only) and require `userId === session.user.id`, with a rate limit on the monthly route. They reject if the user is already premium. The server enforces the plan amount.
    - `/verify-payment` verifies the HMAC signature (`verifyRazorpayPaymentSignature` / `...SubscriptionSignature` in `src/lib/payments/razorpay.js`) and calls `Database.updateUser(subscriptionStatus:'active', isPremium:true, premiumUntil)`.
    - `/webhook` (538) verifies `x-razorpay-signature`, dedupes via `Database.claimWebhookEvent`, and handles `payment.captured`, `subscription.activated|charged|cancelled|halted|completed` and refunds (refund revokes premium).
    - `/api/billing/cancel` + `src/lib/payments/cancel.js` cancel renewal at cycle end. `PlanCard.jsx` is the UI.
  - **How premium is checked:** `data/db.js` `userOut()` computes `isPremium = effectivePremiumFromRow(row)` (`src/lib/payments/entitlements.js`). That is true if `subscription_status==='active'`, or `premium_until` is in the future, or the legacy `is_premium` flag has no expiry. The client reads `user.isPremium`, `premiumUntil` and `razorpaySubscriptionId` from `/api/auth/me`. Server-side gates use `Database.getUserById(...).isPremium`, in `practiceSessions.js` (premium modes, difficulty override), `practice/quote`, `compass/pro` and AI wallet `isPaidUser`.
  - **Credits:** free users spend credits per session via `Database.spendCredits` or the atomic `start_learning_session` RPC (quick 10, full 50); Pro is unmetered. A free daily 10-question set, per-subject 5-question baselines and full-mock samples are tracked in `learning_allowances` (`allowanceKindFor` in `data/practice_quote.js`).
  - **PrepOS top-ups:** `/api/ai/credits/order` and `/verify`, packs Rs 10/50 credits, Rs 20/150, Rs 50/400, gated by `CAPABILITIES.aiTopUps`.
  - **Implication for a native app:** the Razorpay web checkout and all create/verify routes are cookie-auth only. `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `SPEC.md` say the mobile beta ships free with no purchase links, and add that store billing, receipts, account deletion and privacy disclosures are needed before any paid app release.
  
  ## 7. Analytics / events
  
  - **No telemetry SDK** (no GA/GTM, PostHog, Plausible, Sentry, Vercel Analytics, Meta pixel). `src/lib/analytics.js` is an in-memory console logger and **is not imported anywhere** (dead).

===== 2026-10-12T13:46:13.859Z (session 604ced81)
# Workflow authoring reference

A workflow structures work across many agents — to be comprehensive (decompose and cover in parallel), to be confident (independent perspectives and adversarial checks before committing), or to take on scale one context can't hold (migrations, audits, broad sweeps). The script is where you encode that structure: what fans out, what verifies, what synthesizes.

When you do call it, the right move is often **hybrid**: scout inline first (list the files, find the channels, scope the diff) to discover the work-list, then call Workflow to pipeline over it. You don't need to know the shape before the *task* — only before the *orchestration step*.

Common single-phase workflows you can chain across turns:
- **Understand** — parallel readers over relevant subsystems → structured map
- **Design** — judge panel of N independent approaches → scored synthesis
- **Review** — dimensions → find → adversarially verify (the review-changes example)
- **Research** — multi-modal sweep → deep-read → synthesize
- **Migrate** — discover sites → transform each (worktree isolation) → verify

For larger work, run several in sequence — read each result before deciding the next phase. You stay in the loop; each workflow is one well-scoped fan-out.

**Ultracode.** When a system-reminder confirms ultracode is on, that opt-in is standing: author and run a workflow for every substantive task by default. The goal is the most exhaustive, correct answer you can produce — token cost is not a constraint. For multi-phase work (understand → design → implement → review), that often means several workflows in sequence — one per phase — so you stay in the loop between them. The quality patterns below (adversarial verify, multi-modal sweep, completeness critic, loop-until-dry) are the tools; pick what fits the task. Lean toward orchestrating with workflows and adversarially verifying your findings — unless the work is trivial or already verified. Solo only on conversational turns or trivial mechanical edits. When a reminder says ultracode is off, revert to the opt-in rule in the Workflow tool description.

Pass the script inline via `script` — do not Write it to a file first. Every invocation automatically persists its script to a file under the session directory and returns the path in the tool result. To iterate on a workflow, edit that file with Write/Edit and re-invoke Workflow with `{scriptPath: "<path>"}` instead of resending the full script.

Every script must begin with `export const meta = {...}`:
  export const meta = {
    name: 'find-flaky-tests',
    description: 'Find flaky tests and propose fixes',   // one-line, shown in permission dialog
    phases: [                                            // one entry per phase() call
      { title: 'Scan', detail: 'grep test logs for retries' },
      { title: 'Fix', detail: 'one agent per flaky test' },
    ],
  }
  // script body starts here — use agent()/parallel()/pipeline()/phase()/log()
  phase('Scan')
  const flaky = await agent('grep CI logs for retry markers', {schema: FLAKY_SCHEMA})
  ...

The `meta` object must be a PURE LITERAL — no variables, function calls, spreads, or template interpolation. Required fields: `name`, `description`. Optional: `whenToUse` (shown in the workflow list), `phases`. Use the SAME phase titles in meta.phases as in phase() calls — titles are matched exactly; a phase() call with no matching meta entry just gets its own progress group. Add `model` to a phase entry when that phase uses a specific model override.

Script body hooks:
- agent(prompt: string, opts?: {label?: string, phase?: string, schema?: object, model?: string, effort?: string, isolation?: 'worktree', agentType?: string}): Promise<any> — spawn a subagent. Without schema, returns its final text as a string. With schema (a JSON Schema), the subagent is forced to call a StructuredOutput tool and agent() returns the validated object — no parsing needed. Returns null if the user skips the agent mid-run or the subagent dies on a terminal API error after retries (filter with .filter(Boolean)). opts.label overrides the display label. opts.phase explicitly assigns this agent to a progress group (use this inside pipeline()/parallel() stages to avoid races on the global phase() state — same phase string → same group box). opts.model overrides the model for this agent call. Default to omitting it — the agent inherits the main-loop model (the resolved session model), which is almost always correct. Only set it when you're highly confident a different tier fits the task; when unsure, omit. opts.effort overrides the reasoning effort for this agent call ('low' | 'medium' | 'high' | 'xhigh' | 'max') — omit to inherit the session effort; use 'low' for cheap mechanical stages and higher tiers only for the hardest verify/judge stages. opts.isolation: 'worktree' runs the agent in a fresh git worktree — EXPENSIVE (~200-500ms setup + disk per agent), use ONLY when agents mutate files in parallel and would otherwise conflict; the worktree is auto-removed if unchanged. opts.agentType uses a custom subagent type (e.g. 'general-purpose', 'code-reviewer') instead of the default workflow subagent — resolved from the same registry as the Agent tool; composes with schema (the custom agent's system prompt gets a StructuredOutput instruction appended).
- pipeline(items, stage1, stage2, ...): Promise<any[]> — run each item through all stages independently, NO barrier between stages. Item A can be in stage 3 while item B is still in stage 1. This is the DEFAULT for multi-stage work. Wall-clock = slowest single-item chain, not sum-of-slowest-per-stage. Every stage callback receives (prevResult, originalItem, index) — use originalItem/index in later stages to label work without threading context through stage 1's return value. A stage that throws drops that item to `null` and skips its remaining stages.
- parallel(thunks: Array<() => Promise<any>>): Promise<any[]> — run tasks concurrently. This is a BARRIER: awaits all thunks before returning. A thunk that throws (or whose agent errors) resolves to `null` in the result array — the call itself never rejects, so `.filter(Boolean)` before using the results. Use ONLY when you genuinely need all results together.
- log(message: string): void — emit a progress message to the user (shown as a narrator line above the progress tree)
- phase(title: string): void — start a new phase; subsequent agent() calls are grouped under this title in the progress display
- args: any — the value passed as Workflow's `args` input, verbatim (undefined if not provided). Pass arrays/objects as actual JSON values in the tool call, NOT as a JSON-encoded string — `args: ["a.ts", "b.ts"]`, not `args: "[\"a.ts\", ...]"` (a stringified list reaches the script as one string, so `args.filter`/`args.map` throw). Use this to parameterize named workflows — e.g. pass a research question, target path, or config object directly instead of via a side-channel file.
- budget: {total: number|null, spent(): number, remaining(): number} — the turn's token target from the user's "+500k"-style directive. `budget.total` is null if no target was set. `budget.spent()` returns output tokens spent this turn across the main loop and all workflows — the pool is shared, not per-workflow. `budget.remaining()` returns `max(0, total - spent())`, or `Infinity` if no target. The target is a HARD ceiling, not advisory: once `spent()` reaches `total`, further `agent()` calls throw. Use for dynamic loops: `while (budget.total && budget.remaining() > 50_000) { ... }`, or static scaling: `const FLEET = budget.total ? Math.floor(budget.total / 100_000) : 5`.
- workflow(nameOrRef: string | {scriptPath: string}, args?: any): Promise<any> — run another workflow inline as a sub-step and return whatever it returns. Pass a name to invoke a saved workflow (same registry as {name: "..."}), or {scriptPath} to run a script file you Wrote earlier. The child shares this run's concurrency cap, agent counter, abort signal, and token budget — its agents appear under a "▸ name" group in /workflows and its tokens count toward budget.spent(). The args param becomes the child's `args` global. Nesting is one level only: workflow() inside a child throws. Throws on unknown name / unreadable scriptPath / child syntax error; catch to handle gracefully.

Subagents are told their final text IS the return value (not a human-facing message), so they return raw data. For structured output, use the schema option — validation happens at the tool-call layer so the model retries on mismatch.
Schemas need {type: 'object', properties: {...}} at root and required ⊆ properties; unsatisfiable ones throw at agent().

Workflow agents can reach all session-connected MCP tools via ToolSearch — schemas load on demand per agent. Caveat: interactively-authenticated MCP servers (e.g. claude.ai) may be absent in headless/cron runs.

Subagents get the same CLAUDE.md files injected at start that you did (except built-in agent types that omit them, such as Explore and Plan) — don't tell them to re-read those or paste their rules into the prompt; name the specific rule a stage needs, if any.

Scripts are plain JavaScript, NOT TypeScript — type annotations (`: string[]`), interfaces, and generics fail to parse. The script body runs in an async context — use await directly. Standard JS built-ins (JSON, Math, Array, etc.) are available — EXCEPT `Date.now()`/`Math.random()`/argless `new Date()`, which throw (they would break resume); pass timestamps in via `args`, stamp results after the workflow returns, and for randomness vary the agent prompt/label by index. No filesystem or Node.js API access.

DEFAULT TO pipeline(). Only reach for a barrier (parallel between stages) when you genuinely need ALL prior-stage results together.

A barrier is correct ONLY when stage N needs cross-item context from all of stage N-1:
- Dedup/merge across the full result set before expensive downstream work
- Early-exit if the total count is zero ("0 bugs found → skip verification entirely")
- Stage N's prompt references "the other findings" for comparison

A barrier is NOT justified by:
- "I need to flatten/map/filter first" — do it inside a pipeline stage: pipeline(items, stageA, r => transform([r]).flat(), stageB)
- "The stages are conceptually separate" — that's what pipeline() models. Separate stages ≠ synchronized stages.
- "It's cleaner code" — barrier latency is real. If 5 finders run and the slowest takes 3× the fastest, a barrier wastes 2/3 of the fast finders' idle time.

Smell test: if you wrote
  const a = await parallel(...)
  const b = transform(a)        // flatten, map, filter — no cross-item dependency
  const c = await parallel(b.map(...))
that middle transform doesn't need the barrier. Rewrite as a pipeline with the transform inside a stage. When in doubt: pipeline.

Concurrent agent() calls are capped at min(16, available CPUs - 2) per workflow — excess calls queue and run as slots free up. You can still pass 100 items to parallel()/pipeline() and they all complete; only ~10 run at any moment. Total agent count across a workflow's lifetime is capped at 1000 — a runaway-loop backstop set far above any real workflow. A single parallel()/pipeline() call accepts at most 4096 items; passing more is an explicit error, not a silent truncation.

When a barrier IS correct — dedup across all findings before expensive verification:
  const all = await parallel(DIMENSIONS.map(d => () => agent(d.prompt, {schema: FINDINGS_SCHEMA})))
  const deduped = dedupeByFileAndLine(all.filter(Boolean).flatMap(r => r.findings))  // <-- genuinely needs ALL at once
  const verified = await parallel(deduped.map(f => () => agent(verifyPrompt(f), {schema: VERDICT_SCHEMA})))

Loop-until-count pattern — accumulate to a target:
  const bugs = []
  while (bugs.length < 10) {
    const result = await agent("Find bugs in this codebase.", {schema: BUGS_SCHEMA})
    bugs.push(...result.bugs)
    log(`${bugs.length}/10 found`)
  }

Loop-until-budget pattern — scale depth to the user's "+500k" directive. Guard on budget.total: with no target set, remaining() is Infinity and the loop would run straight to the 1000-agent cap.
  const bugs = []
  while (budget.total && budget.remaining() > 50_000) {
    const result = await agent("Find bugs in this codebase.", {schema: BUGS_SCHEMA})
    bugs.push(...result.bugs)
    log(`${bugs.length} found, ${Math.round(budget.remaining()/1000)}k remaining`)
  }

Composing patterns — exhaustive review (find → dedup vs seen → diverse-lens panel → loop-until-dry):
  const seen = new Set(), confirmed = []
  let dry = 0
  while (dry < 2) {                                              // loop-until-dry
    const found = (await parallel(FINDERS.map(f => () =>          // barrier: collect all finders this round
      agent(f.prompt, {phase: 'Find', schema: BUGS})))).filter(Boolean).flatMap(r => r.bugs)
    const fresh = found.filter(b => !seen.has(key(b)))           // dedup vs ALL seen — plain code, not an agent
    if (!fresh.length) { dry++; continue }
    dry = 0; fresh.forEach(b => seen.add(key(b)))
    const judged = await parallel(fresh.map(b => () =>           // every fresh bug judged concurrently...
      parallel(['correctness','security','repro'].map(lens => () =>   // ...each by 3 distinct lenses
        agent(`Judge "${b.desc}" via the ${lens} lens — real?`, {phase: 'Verify', schema: VERDICT})))
        .then(vs => ({ b, real: vs.filter(Boolean).filter(v => v.real).length >= 2 }))))
    confirmed.push(...judged.filter(v => v.real).map(v => v.b))
  }
  return confirmed
  // dedup vs `seen`, NOT `confirmed` — else judge-rejected findings reappear every round and it never converges.

Quality patterns — common shapes; pick by task and compose freely:
- Adversarial verify: spawn N independent skeptics per finding, each prompted to REFUTE. Kill if ≥majority refute. Prevents plausible-but-wrong findings from surviving.
    const votes = await parallel(Array.from({length: 3}, () => () =>
      agent(`Try to refute: ${claim}. Default to refuted=true if uncertain.`, {schema: VERDICT})))
    const survives = votes.filter(Boolean).filter(v => !v.refuted).length >= 2
- Perspective-diverse verify: when a finding can fail in more than one way, give each verifier a distinct lens (correctness, security, perf, does-it-reproduce) instead of N identical refuters — diversity catches failure modes redundancy can't.
- Judge panel: generate N independent attempts from different angles (e.g. MVP-first, risk-first, user-first), score with parallel judges, synthesize from the winner while grafting the best ideas from runners-up. Beats one-attempt-iterated when the solution space is wide.
- Loop-until-dry: for unknown-size discovery (bugs, issues, edge cases), keep spawning finders until K consecutive rounds return nothing new. Simple counters (while count < N) miss the tail.
- Multi-modal sweep: parallel agents each searching a different way (by-container, by-content, by-entity, by-time). Each is blind to what the others surface; useful when one search angle won't find everything.
- Completeness critic: a final agent that asks "what's missing — modality not run, claim unverified, source unread?" What it finds becomes the next round of work.
- No silent caps: if a workflow bounds coverage (top-N, no-retry, sampling), `log()` what was dropped — silent truncation reads as "covered everything" when it didn't.

Scale to what the user asked for. "find any bugs" → a few finders, single-vote verify. "thoroughly audit this" or "be comprehensive" → larger finder pool, 3–5 vote adversarial pass, synthesis stage. When unsure, lean toward thoroughness for research/review/audit requests and toward brevity for quick checks.

These patterns aren't exhaustive — compose novel harnesses when the task calls for it (tournament brackets, self-repair loops, staged escalation, whatever fits).

Use this tool for multi-step orchestration where control flow should be deterministic (loops, conditionals, fan-out) rather than model-driven.

## Resume

The tool result includes a runId. To resume after a pause, kill, or script edit, relaunch with Workflow({scriptPath, resumeFromRunId}) — the longest unchanged prefix of agent() calls returns cached results instantly; the first edited/new call and everything after it runs live. Same script + same args → 100% cache hit. Before diagnosing why a completed workflow returned an empty or unexpected result, Read <transcriptDir>/journal.jsonl — it records each agent's actual return value; do not assume cached results are non-empty. Date.now()/Math.random()/new Date() are unavailable in scripts (they would break this) — stamp results after the workflow returns, or pass timestamps via args. Fallback when no journal is available: Read agent-<id>.jsonl files in the transcript directory and hand-author a continuation script.

# UI/UX standards (the owner's)

The owner wants every model to take UI/UX and the whole experience seriously on every UI task, without being reminded. Before writing or changing UI:

1. **Read the full standards:** `C:\Users\atish\Desktop\AI OS\wiki\concepts\ui-ux-standards.md`. It is about 3,000 words and every rule is sourced. For a tiny copy or style fix, the digest below is enough.
2. **Read the project's own system first,** if one exists: its design docs, tokens and components. It overrides the generic choices here. Project addenda are in section 12 of the standards. MockMob's is its docs/brain/PRODUCT-EXPERIENCE-RESEARCH-2026-10-12.md; Continuum's is audits/v6/DESIGN-FINAL.md in the AI OS folder.
3. **For a new screen or journey, write the short experience brief** (section 1) before coding.
4. **Before calling it done, run the done check** (section 11): exercise it in a real browser at real sizes, look at the screenshots, check the slop list (section 10), and say exactly what you verified.

## Digest
1. Brief first: the user's job, one primary action, which data is real, every state (loading, empty, error, sparse, success, return), and how you will prove it works.
2. Data decides the form. Units and denominators go beside counts; zero is not unknown; no false precision; real numbers only, each with context (trend, age or source).
3. The first thing seen answers the user's question. One dominant action, one information-carrying anchor, whitespace over boxes, never nested cards, nothing floating over content.
4. Type: one chosen family, few sizes, tight large headings, sentence-case labels (no ALL-CAPS eyebrows), tabular numerals (no monospace labels), 12-hour time.
5. Colour comes from meaning: one accent; no decorative gradients, glow or glass; neutral charcoal dark mode (not #000, not tinted); never colour alone; soft shadows.
6. Design every control state, focus included. Never show success before the system confirms it; no fake progress; errors give a way to recover.
7. Copy is plain and concrete: no hype, AI cliches, exclamation marks, emoji, arrows appended to buttons, or build talk (codes, ports, file names).
8. Motion only answers an action or shows state: ease-out, 150-350 ms, reduced motion respected, never hiding a slow flow.
9. WCAG 2.2 AA, 44 px touch targets, visible focus, no layout shift.
10. Done means: exercised in a real browser at real sizes, screenshots looked at, the slop list checked, and you say exactly what you verified.

Source of truth: the standards page above (in the Continuum brain at C:\Users\atish\Desktop\AI OS). If this skill and the page ever differ, the page wins. You can also search the brain with continuum_search "ui standards".

  Review of `git diff 5f7d53e 5d79433` is done. I fixed six small defects and committed them as **6e68228** on fix/web-state-home. Nothing is pushed or deployed, and I did not run `next build` or `next dev`.
  
  ## Defects found and fixed
  (file:line are in 5d79433)
  1. **Dead link for members.** `src/app/page.js:121`: the "CUET UG 2027" pill links to `#try-practice`, which is hidden for signed-in students. Members now get a plain label instead of a link, via a `vs-static` class in `visitor.css`.
  2. **Wrong reading and tab order.** `src/app/page.js:150`: `<MemberHeroCopy />` sat after the DU calculator link and the exam link. A member saw the calculator promo above "Welcome back", and tab reached it before "Enter the Arena". I moved it to just after `ReturningNote`. Guests are unaffected because the member parts are hidden for them.
  3. **Long first name clipped at 375 px.** `VisitorHero.jsx:56`, the member heading. I measured with the real Gabarito font on the live page. "Venkatasubramanian" overflowed the column by 9 px, and a 24-character name by 116 px. The html and body clip overflow, so the name would be cut off. I added `.lp .lp-title.vs-title { overflow-wrap: anywhere }` in `visitor.css`. With it, both names stay inside the column and wrap to 4 lines. Normal names ("Welcome back, Atish." plus the title) take 3 lines, the same height as the guest title.
  4. **Offline and submit-waiting notice may not be read out.** `RunnerNotice.jsx:11`: the `role="status"` element was only inserted when a notice existed, and a status region inserted already filled is often not announced. The region now stays in the page and only its content changes. The base runner has no flex or grid layout, so an empty wrapper adds no space.
  5. **Over-promise in the submit-waiting copy.** `data/runner_notice.mjs:14` said "Reconnect and they will be sent". `TestPageClient` only auto-resubmits within `endsAt` plus `SUBMISSION_GRACE_MS` (2 minutes). It now says: "Reconnect soon: they send on their own while the submission window is open." I added a test assertion for it.
  6. **Lint errors in the new code.** `VisitorHero.jsx:21` and `:37` fail `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`. I used the same `queueMicrotask` pattern as `ResultPageClient`. I also removed an unused eslint-disable in `VisitorSync.jsx:35`. ESLint is now clean on all changed files.
  
  ## Checked, no defect
  - **Hydration and flash.** `<html>` has `suppressHydrationWarning`. `data-visitor` is not in the server markup, and React never overwrites it. The member components render the same on server and first client render. CSS uses `!important` and the boot script runs in `<head>`, so first paint is correct.
  - **CSP.** There is no CSP or nonce anywhere (`next.config.mjs`, `proxy.js`, `vercel.json`, `src/`). The new inline script behaves like the existing theme script. If a CSP is added later, both scripts will need a nonce.
  - **Redirect.** It needs preference `'1'`, a confirmed `authenticated` status, no `?home`, no same-origin referrer, and a landing path of `/`. It fires once per page load (`arenaRedirected`), so there is no loop. Crawlers and signed-out visitors have no localStorage, so they never redirect.
  - **Sign-out.** Both `signOut()` and `SIGNED_OUT` go through `applyUnauthenticated`, which sets status to `unauthenticated`. `VisitorSync` then rewrites the hint to `returning` (no plan or name stored) and sets `data-visitor`. `clearStudyDrafts` removes the resume drafts.
  - **Plan.** `/api/auth/me` returns `isPremium`, `premiumUntil` and a numeric `creditBalance`, which match the copy.
  - **SEO.** The guest h1, lead, CTAs and proof strip are in the server HTML, and member and returning parts are `display:none` without a stored hint. `LandingActions` and `MobileDock` are now server components and `Icon` is server-safe.
  - **375 px dock.** I did not measure it. Someone else's session took over the shared browser tab before I got to it. The "Enter the Arena" label is shorter than the "Try a CUET question" label already used there.
  - **Themes.** All tokens in `visitor.css` resolve in both light and dark. The runner uses `--text-subtle` and `--text-primary`, which both consoles define.
  - **Notice states.** Offline, submit waiting and sync pending are each covered by tests. The audit disclaimer is gone from the test screen.
  
  ## For the owner to decide
  1. **"Returning" is too broad.** `data/visitor_state.mjs:65`: `nextHint` marks every signed-out first visit as `returning`, so on their second page load a visitor who never made an account sees "Welcome back. Sign in to pick up your mistakes, your Radar…". "Sign in" becomes their primary button and "Start free practice" leaves the hero. A reload of home on a first visit triggers it too. I suggest recording `returning` only after a `member` hint (a sign-out), but that changes tested behaviour, so I left it.
  2. **One-off wrong hero at deploy.** Students already signed in have no hint yet, so their first load shows the marketing hero for the 0.3 to 2 seconds the auth check takes, then flips to the member hero. The `sb-…-auth-token` cookie could seed the boot script.
  3. **Disclaimer moved, not removed.** `ResultPageClient` now shows "These questions come from our practice bank, which we are still checking…" under every result for practice sessions, which are all `legacy_bank` at the moment. Your design direction says no obvious disclaimers.
  4. **Mid-page CTAs for members.** The home pricing "Start free" button, the "Try Mistake Repair" tour slide and the FAQ "Can I try it before signing up?" are unchanged for members. `/signup` redirects signed-in users.
  5. **Small leftovers, not touched.**
     - `mm:arena-first:v1` survives sign-out, so another student on a shared device would inherit it.
     - The NavBar sheet uses `isAuthenticated`, so it can show "Log in" to a member during loading or `unavailable`.
     - The offline banner pushes the test content down by about 44 px when connectivity drops. The old banner was always present.
     - The mobile dock says "Pick up where you left off." on every page, even with no test to resume.
  
  ## Test results
  - `node --test data/tests/visitor_state.test.mjs data/tests/runner_notice.test.mjs`: 21 of 21 pass. This is after my edits and includes the new assertion.
  - `npm run test:recovery`: 39 of 39 pass.
  - ESLint on all changed files: clean.
  
  I briefly used the shared Browser pane tab on the live site for the font measurement. Another session has since moved that tab to localhost:3107.

  **Policy as implemented** (`lib/features/notifications/logic/pre_prompt_policy.dart`)
  - **Spacing:** the first ask is due at once. After the 1st ask the next waits 2 days, after the 2nd 4 days, after the 3rd 7 days, and after every later ask 10 days.
  - **Once a day:** there is a hard floor of 1 day between asks whatever the count says. A last-ask time in the future (clock moved back) reads as not yet. A count with no saved date is due, and the next ask records the date.
  - **Moments:** only the new `PrePromptMoment.afterResult` may ask. `inSession`, `ntaMode` and `appOpen` always return false. Granted or unavailable never asks. `denied` (blocked) is still offered.
  - **Persistence:** the time stays in `AppPrefs.lastPrePromptAt`. The count stays in `ReminderState` (JSON key `n`), now also readable as `prePromptAskCount`. `AppPrefs` is outside the allowed folders, so I did not add keys there.
  - **What counts as an ask:** opening the sheet counts as one ask, written before it can close, so Not now, swipe, back or a refused system dialog all keep the spacing and never double count. The recap row's Not now still counts one, as before. A sheet opened with reminders already on counts nothing. Opening from Settings > Turn on also counts as an ask.
  - **Providers:** `prePromptDueProvider` keeps its zero-argument signature and means "after a result". The new `prePromptDueAtProvider(moment)` takes a `PrePromptMoment`. I removed the old rule that stopped for good once the system question had been asked or Not now was pressed twice.
  - **Android fallback:** the existing refusal counter (`AndroidPermissionRequester`) already reports `denied` after two refusals. `prePromptPrimaryFor(denied)` then makes the main key "Open settings", which opens notification settings and never calls the system request.

  - **Trigger:** `lib/features/result/recap/recap_flow.dart`, started when the sealed sheet opens. It waits 4 s (`RecapFlow.reviewAskDelay`), checks `mounted`, is cancelled in `dispose`, and calls `reviewPromptProvider.maybeAsk(moment, ntaMode: m.isNta)`.
  - **Which results ask:** `RecapModel.reviewMoment` (in `lib/features/result/model/recap_model.dart`) returns:
    - `resultImproved` for a full mock with tone `higher`.

  WIP 2209123 is finished, together with audit items R1 to R3, in commit **fb510e5** on feat/app-and-web. I have not pushed it.
  
  **Results**
  - `flutter analyze` reports no issues.
  - The full suite (`bash tool/flutter_locked.sh test -j 1 -r failures-only`, MOCKMOB_FLUTTER_SLOTS=1) ended with **+3153: All tests passed!** The last green run before this was +2990.
  - My first full run ended +3147 -6. The 6 were You goldens whose mock `post`/`patch` stubs had no `expectUserId` matcher. After fixing the stubs they pass against the existing images, unchanged, and the second full run was fully green.
  
  **Bugs found in the WIP**
  1. **Session calls had no guard, so a student's unsent paper could be lost (R1).** After an account switch, the events PUT, resume, submit and attempt read went out with the new account's token. A 403 or 404 from the server then marked the first student's draft rejected or window-closed.
  2. **The background job refused every named write.** The headless token source could never say whose token it held. Had R1 and R2 used the guard there, every background submit and outbox flush would have been refused.
  3. **A 401 retry could resend the refused token.** If the session ended during the refresh, the call was repeated with the old token instead of failing as signed out.
  4. **Outbox replays named no owner (R2).**
  5. **Account deletion kept the unsent onboarding sheet.** It stayed parked on the phone for 30 days, although the deletion copy promises unsent answers are removed (R3).
  6. **Test bugs from the WIP:**
     - Apostrophes inside single-quoted test names broke `api_client_test.dart` and `sheet_sync_test.dart` (the "broken group blocks").
     - The new prompt-fit test expected "Allow reminders" for a denied permission. A denied permission always shows "Open settings", so I corrected the expectation, not the app.
  
  **Per item**
  1. **Expected-user guard**
     - `ApiClient.get` can now name its account too.
     - The foreground token source reads the Supabase user from the token's own JWT `sub`, falling back to matching the session, and maps it to the account whose profile was loaded for that user.
     - For the background job, `_applyProfile` now records which account belongs to which Supabase user (new prefs key `session_account`), and `BackgroundTokenRefresher` reads it. A token it cannot name has its named calls refused, and they are retried later, never dropped.
     - `_profileSessionUser` is cleared at sign-out.
     - R1: `SessionApi.submit` and `fetchAttempt` take `forUserId`; the submitter passes `draft.userId`. `ApiSessionApi(api, userId:)` is used for the events PUT and resume, in both the foreground and background. A guard refusal is not a definitive refusal, so the submit stays `SubmitPending`.
     - R2: every outbox row already has a non-null `user_id` (it has been there since the table was created), so there are no legacy rows without an owner. `OutboxItem.userId` now carries it and the replay passes it. A refusal keeps the item and stops the flush. An item built by hand with no owner behaves as before, scoped to whoever flushes it; there is a comment on this.
  2. **Attempts list fetch time and Today's "Updated" line:** reviewed, correct as written. Added tests.
  3. **`cancelAll()` after `signOutLocal()`:** correct on both sign-out and deletion. Tests check the order (cancel, sign out, cancel again), that a failing second cancel does not stop the sign-out, and that nothing runs after a failed token clear.
  4. **StateAction loading flag:** correct. Added a StateView test (spinner shows, size kept, taps ignored) and a load-failed screen assertion.
  5. **MmSheetPage footer:** correct. I opened old and new images for the 10 changed prompt goldens. The only change is a hairline above the pinned keys and the keys sitting 8 dp lower, which is intended, so I re-accepted them.

  New tests also cover the guard on the client side, foreground and background account naming, `jwtSubject`, the outbox, and session submit and attempt reads. I fixed every broken WIP fake and mock stub without weakening an existing assertion. Delete, name and subjects verifies now check `expectUserId: 'u1'`.

  Nothing was changed: no code, no database writes, no model calls. The 12 SELECT-only queries ran without errors against a local in-memory copy built from the real migrations. They have not been run on production, so every likelihood below comes from reading the code.
  
  **Top three failure causes**
  1. **Nothing limits how long a repair takes, and a killed repair keeps the student's credit.**
     - The route has no `maxDuration`, and the OpenAI client has no timeout (the SDK default is 10 minutes).

# Building LLM-Powered Applications with Claude

This skill helps you build LLM-powered applications with Claude. Choose the right surface based on your needs, detect the project language, then read the relevant language-specific documentation.

## Before You Start

Scan the target file (or, if no target file, the prompt and project) for non-Anthropic provider markers - `import openai`, `from openai`, `langchain_openai`, `OpenAI(`, `gpt-4`, `gpt-5`, file names like `agent-openai.py` or `*-generic.py`, or any explicit instruction to keep the code provider-neutral. If you find any, stop and tell the user that this skill produces Claude/Anthropic SDK code; ask whether they want to switch the file to Claude or want a non-Claude implementation. Do not edit a non-Anthropic file with Anthropic SDK calls. (Exception: the `prompt-audit` subcommand is non-interactive and does not stop here - it records non-Anthropic provider markers in its report's stated assumptions and never proposes switching a non-Anthropic file to the Anthropic SDK.)

## Output Requirement

When the user asks you to add, modify, or implement a Claude feature, your code must call Claude through one of:

1. **The official Anthropic SDK** for the project's language (`anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `com.anthropic.*`, etc.). This is the default whenever a supported SDK exists for the project.
2. **Raw HTTP** (`curl`, `requests`, `fetch`, `httpx`, etc.) - only when the user explicitly asks for cURL/REST/raw HTTP, the project is a shell/cURL project, or the language has no official SDK.

Never mix the two - don't reach for `requests`/`fetch` in a Python or TypeScript project just because it feels lighter. Never fall back to OpenAI-compatible shims.

**Never guess SDK usage.** Function names, class names, namespaces, method signatures, and import paths must come from explicit documentation - either the `{lang}/` files in this skill or the official SDK repositories or documentation links listed in `shared/live-sources.md`. If the binding you need is not explicitly documented in the skill files, WebFetch the relevant SDK repo from `shared/live-sources.md` before writing code. Do not infer Ruby/Java/Go/PHP/C# APIs from cURL shapes or from another language's SDK.

**If WebFetch or repository access fails** (network restricted, timeouts, clone blocked): do not keep retrying - write code from the patterns and namespace/package tables in the `{lang}/` file, run the compiler or interpreter on it, and iterate on the error output. For statically-typed SDKs (C#, Java, Go) a compile-fix loop against local errors reaches working code faster than blocked network research.

## Defaults

Unless the user requests otherwise:

For the Claude model version, please use Claude Opus 5.5, which you can access via the exact model string `claude-opus-5-5`. Please default to using adaptive thinking (`thinking: {type: "adaptive"}`) for anything remotely complicated. And finally, please default to streaming for any request that may involve long input, long output, or high `max_tokens` - it prevents hitting request timeouts. Use the SDK's `.get_final_message()` / `.finalMessage()` helper to get the complete response if you don't need to handle individual stream events. When a streaming request defines user-defined (client) tools, set `eager_input_streaming: true` on each of those tools so large tool inputs (file contents, code, documents) stream as they are generated instead of arriving in one burst after the server finishes buffering them; the client then owns validation: the SDKs' tolerant parsers can return a silently truncated input instead of raising, so validate each parsed tool input against its schema before running it (the typed runner helpers such as `betaZodTool` / typed `@beta_tool` do this; `betaTool()` JSON-Schema tools and manual loops must validate themselves), treat a failure like invalid JSON (`INVALID_JSON` error `tool_result` when you hold the block, re-issue otherwise), check `max_tokens` / `refusal` stop reasons before running tools, and catch only the SDK's JSON error, never its typed API errors - pattern in `shared/tool-use-concepts.md` -> Eager input streaming. Leave it off for non-streaming requests, for server tools, and when the request goes through a proxy or an older Bedrock model deployment that rejects the field.

## Warning: API Drift - Your Training Prior May Be Stale

Several common Claude API shapes changed in 2025-2026. If you recall a pattern from training, verify it against the `{lang}/` files in this skill before writing - the rows below are the most frequent drift points:

| Area | Stale prior | Current API |
|---|---|---|
| Extended thinking | `thinking: {type: "enabled", budget_tokens: N}` | On Claude 4.6+ models: `thinking: {type: "adaptive"}`. `budget_tokens` is deprecated on Opus 4.6 / Sonnet 4.6 and **rejected with a 400** on Fable 5/5.1 / Sonnet 5.5 / Sonnet 5 / Opus 5.5 / 5 / 4.8 / 4.7. Pre-4.6 models still use `budget_tokens`. |
| Web search / web fetch tool type | `web_search_20250305`, `web_fetch_20250910` | `web_search_20260209`, `web_fetch_20260209` (dynamic filtering) on Opus 5.5/5/4.8/4.7/4.6, Sonnet 5.5, Sonnet 5, and Sonnet 4.6. Older models keep the basic variants; on Vertex AI only basic `web_search_20250305` is available (web fetch is not on Vertex) - see the Server Tools QR below. |
| PHP parameter names | snake_case wire names as named args (`max_tokens`) | Top-level named args are camelCase (`maxTokens`). Nested array keys vary by feature (e.g. `'taskBudget'`, `'skillID'`, `'mcp_server_name'`) - copy the exact key from the documented example; do not bulk-convert. |
| Managed Agents credentials | Keep secrets host-side via custom tools (the only option before vaults shipped) | Vault `environment_variable` credentials - stored by Anthropic, substituted at egress, never visible in the sandbox (`shared/managed-agents-tools.md` -> Vaults). Host-side custom tools remain the fallback for self-hosted sandboxes. |
| Files API / Skills | `client.beta.files.*` / `client.beta.skills.*` with beta `files-api-2025-04-14` / `skills-2025-10-02` | Out of beta: `client.files.*` / `client.skills.*`, no beta header. In current SDKs `client.beta.files` / `client.beta.skills` have breaking shape changes from previous versions, matching the stable namespaces - migrate per `shared/live-sources.md` -> Files API / Skills Guide. |

The `{lang}/` files in this skill are authoritative over recalled patterns.
